## Findings, written as each unit is read

### F-01 (Q6, Q3): `tools/retroarch-wrapper-test --gated` asserts nothing

`tools/retroarch-wrapper-test:57` (after #799, line numbers at `d606301784`) sets `gated = "--gated" in sys.argv`, and no other line reads `gated` (`grep -n gated` prints the usage line 7 and line 57 only). `git log -S gated` shows the flag arrived with the tool's first commit, `e5d14d4220` (2026-09-19), already unread. A `--gated` run prints exactly the plain run's lines (above). The docstring does not say what it was meant to assert. The brief told the lane to read the docstring before trusting either line, and the docstring is silent. **So the usage line advertises a mode that does not exist, and a reader who runs `--gated` believes something extra was checked.** Fix shape for the punch list: remove the flag and its usage mention, or implement what the author meant (the history does not say, so it is a question for the coordinator). Outside the membership, in the slice's group 5.

### F-02 (Q2, Q6): `sameboy-lr`'s `BOOTROMS_DIR` is a late-binding violation and a dead option

`projects/ROCKNIX/packages/emulators/libretro/sameboy-lr/package.mk:13` reads `PKG_MAKE_OPTS_TARGET="-C libretro BOOTROMS_DIR=${PKG_BUILD}/BootROMs/prebuilt"` at global scope. `PKG_BUILD` is empty there (`packages/README.md` § Late binding), so the option is `BOOTROMS_DIR=/BootROMs/prebuilt`. It is also read by nothing on this build path:

- the recipe builds with `-C libretro`;
- SameBoy's `libretro/Makefile` (the M7 GENERIC_X64 root's unpack of `aa158a889a`, read-only) takes its boot ROMs from `$(CORE_DIR)/BootROMs/prebuilt/%_boot.bin` (its lines 368 and 381);
- only the top-level `Makefile` reads `BOOTROMS_DIR` (line 56), for the SDL, tester and app targets.

The core is built and installed regardless (`image/system/usr/lib/libretro/sameboy_libretro.so`, 355,104 bytes). **So it is the `ppsspp-lr` shape of `device-builds.md` § Late binding bites hardest in a merge, with no effect today: a variable that is empty, passed to a make that ignores it.** `pkgcheck` fails the recipe on it. History: the line came with the ROCKNIX recipe (`9f1fab30f6`, 2025-07-13), unchanged since. `upstream/next` carries the same line (its only diff is the dependency line of `af70bd435b`). Fix shape: drop `BOOTROMS_DIR=...`, keep `-C libretro`. Proof: `pkgcheck sameboy-lr` rc 0; the core's build has no image until the coordinator's integration build, so its row is `fix-pending-image`. An upstream contribution candidate for #585 (D-WORKFLOW-164).

### F-03 (Q2, Q7): `flycast2021-lr` assigns `PKG_MAKE_OPTS_TARGET` between two functions

`flycast2021-lr/package.mk:32` (`PKG_MAKE_OPTS_TARGET+=" ARCH=${TARGET_ARCH} HAVE_OPENMP=0 GIT_VERSION=${PKG_VERSION:0:7} HAVE_LTCG=0"`) sits between `post_unpack()` and `pre_make_target()`. `pkgcheck` warns that this is cosmetic. It is also a late-binding reference: `TARGET_ARCH` is set by the build system before recipes load (`config/options` reads `config/arch.${TARGET_ARCH}`), so it is not on the README's late-binding list, and `pkgcheck` does not fail it. The recipe is upstream-carried and in `X64_SKIP_CORES`. Cosmetic Q2 only: the fix is to move the line above the functions. It is a Q7 row, not a defect.

### The launch table on the image, parsed (03:03 UTC)

`usr/config/emulationstation/es_systems.cfg` from the M7 GENERIC_X64 build root's install tree (83,206 bytes), parsed with `python3 -I` and `xml.etree.ElementTree`:

- **128 `<system>`, 152 `<emulator>`, 348 `<core>`.** The brief's recorded "500 `<core` elements" is a text count that also matches the 152 `<cores>` wrappers: 348 + 152 = 500. **This is a delta, recorded beside the brief's figure.**
- **Commands:** 125 systems carry `RUNCMD` (`/usr/bin/runemu.sh %ROM% -P%SYSTEM% --core=%CORE% --emulator=%EMULATOR% --controllers="%CONTROLLERSCONFIG%"`). Three carry their own: `imageviewer` `/usr/bin/true`, `moonlight` `/bin/sh %ROM%`, `tools` `/usr/bin/foot %ROM%`.
- **Defaults:** every system has exactly one `default="true"` core except four. `imageviewer` and `tools` have no emulator, by design. **`psx` and `st-v` have none** (F-05).

### F-04 (Q2): `Start Azahar.sh` and `Start MelonDS.sh` carry their shebang on line 2

Both files begin with an empty line and put `#!/bin/bash` on line 2 (`head -4 | cat -A`). Both are upstream-carried (`1a30c6a368` 2026-03-23; `02fe535ab1` 2026-03-20), and `upstream/next` has the same bytes. Installed as `/usr/config/modules/<name>.sh` on the devices whose arms call `install_script` (`package.mk` 194, 998 to 1035), they run from the `tools` system as `/usr/bin/foot %ROM%`. `foot` calls `execvp` (`strings usr/bin/foot`). With no shebang on line 1 the kernel refuses the file (ENOEXEC), and glibc's `execvp` falls back to running it with `/bin/sh`. On the image `/bin` is a symlink to `/usr/bin` and `usr/bin/sh` points at `bash` (read in the install tree), so the scripts still run under bash, and `Start MelonDS.sh`'s `[[` works. **So today it is a standards defect with no visible effect, resting on two facts outside the file:** glibc's fallback and the image's `sh`. Neither module is installed on GENERIC_X64 (`usr/config/modules/` lists 19 others), so the guest cannot run the shipped file. Fix shape: delete the blank first line in both. Can this be done on the VM? The fallback interpreter can be shown on the guest with a fixture of the same shape (a blank line, a line-2 shebang, a `[[` test) run through `env`, which also calls `execvp`; that is a lead to prove if the fix is taken.

### F-05 (Q3, Q6): on GENERIC_X64 `psx` has no default core, and `st-v` has no emulator on the fork's handheld images either

`virtual/emulators/package.mk` 1165 to 1209: the `psx` `case ${DEVICE}` has arms for every device except **GENERIC_X64**. After it come two lines for every device: `add_emu_core psx duckstation duckstation-sa false` and `add_emu_core psx retroarch swanstation false`. So on the VM image `psx` carries two emulators and no `default="true"`, as the parsed table shows: `duckstation` (`duckstation-sa`) and `retroarch` (`swanstation`), both unmarked. Which one EmulationStation then launches is the interface's choice (T2's `SystemData` rows). Read there, not assumed here.

`st-v` (1395 to 1401) gets `add_emu_core st-v mednafen ss true` only on RK3588, SM4450, SM6115, SM8250, SM8550, SM8650, SM8750 and AMD64. **So on H700, RK3326, RK3566 and GENERIC_X64, four of the fork's five built devices, the table carries an `st-v` system with no emulator at all** (the image's `st-v` entry has an empty `emulators` list). A player with ST-V games on an H700 sees a system that cannot launch them.

To prove per device, by the host regeneration of the table (the next check), and to read against `upstream/next` (whether the same lines are upstream's) before a fix shape is written. The VM claim is the image's table, read above: `image-set` evidence, read on the host, not a guest proof.

### The host regeneration of the table (03:01 to 03:04 UTC): the tree's own shell, proven byte-exact against three images

`docs/audits/2026_10_24-epic-m6-emulators-libretro/tools/regen-es-systems.sh` (committed with this file) sources `distributions/ROCKNIX/config/functions` and `virtual/emulators/package.mk` from the worktree. It runs `makeinstall_target` under `bash -e`, matching the real build (`config/path:4` sets `set -e`), inside `env -i`. Every write goes under `.qa-scratch/t3-regen/<DEVICE>/`: `ROOT`, `ESTMP`, `INSTALL` and `SYSDOC`, with `ROOT/config` a read-only symlink to the tree's `config/`.

**A gap in the brief's recipe, found and closed:** brief § 5 says only the formatted file needs `xmlstarlet`. But `add_es_system` calls `xmlstarlet sel` and `xmlstarlet ed` for every core (`functions` 171 to 214), so without it every system would come out with no emulators. **The run therefore uses the build's own host `xmlstarlet`**, copied from the M7 GENERIC_X64 root's `toolchain/bin/xml` (`xmlstarlet` is a symlink to it) into `.qa-scratch/t3-regen/xs/xml`. The copy and the original both have sha256 `3ee557da6cbdefa8…`; version 1.6.1, libxml2 2.15.3.

`get_pkg_version` (the build engine's) is a stub that reads `PKG_VERSION` from the recipe, the `projects/ROCKNIX` override first. It logs every call and every miss.

| `DEVICE` (`ARCH`, `ENABLE_32BIT`, `WINDOWMANAGER`, `PREFER_GLES`) | rc | Systems | Against the image built from it | The stub |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- | --- |
| GENERIC_X64 (`x86_64`, `no`, `swaywm-env`, `no`) | 0 | 128 | **byte-identical**: `es_systems.cfg` (sha256 `95b1a9b6b166a47d…`, 83,206 bytes), `system-dirs.conf`, `core-pins` (128 lines); the 8 modules it installs are among the image's 19 (the 11 others come from other packages) | 129 calls, 0 misses |
| H700 (`aarch64`, `true`, `swaywm-env`, `yes`) | 0 | 126 | **byte-identical** to `m7-signin-h700-build-20261023-06`'s install tree: `es_systems.cfg`, `system-dirs.conf`, `core-pins`; its modules all present | 0 misses |
| RK3326 (the same knobs as H700) | 0 | 126 | no image on the host | 0 misses |
| RK3566 (`aarch64`, `true`, `swaywm-env`, `yes`) | 0 | 132 | no image on the host | 0 misses |
| SM8550 (`aarch64`, `true`, `swaywm-env`, `no`) | 0 | 139 | **byte-identical** to `m7-signin-sm8550-build-20261023-03`'s: `es_systems.cfg`, `system-dirs.conf`, `core-pins` | 0 misses |

The device knobs are each device's `options` (`WINDOWMANAGER`, `PREFER_GLES`; GENERIC_X64's `ENABLE_32BIT=no`), and `ENABLE_32BIT` defaults to `true` (`distributions/ROCKNIX/options:69`). The H700 and SM8550 images were built from `08cc7ff6cd`. On the two slice directories, `functions` and `config/emulators`, that source differs from `next` only in `Install Steam.sh` (`git -C /workspace/repos/rocknix diff --stat next 08cc7ff6cd -- ...`), which the table does not read.

**So the regeneration is the build's own table for any tree.** A fix to the recipe or the table can be proven on the host by this script (`fix-host-regen`), and the five devices' tables are the Q6 input. A FAIL it can produce: any edit to an `add_emu_core` line changes the bytes; the constructed case is below.

**The five tables, analysed** (`python3 -I`, `ElementTree`, over each regenerated `es_systems.cfg`; per system: the count of `default="true"` cores and whether it has an emulator):

| Device | Systems | Emulators | Cores | No default | More than one default | No emulator (besides `tools`, `imageviewer`) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | --- | --- |
| H700 | 126 | 184 | 395 | none | none | `st-v` |
| RK3326 | 126 | 184 | 395 | none | none | `st-v` |
| RK3566 | 132 | 196 | 410 | none | none | `st-v` |
| SM8550 | 139 | 256 | 481 | none | none | none |
| GENERIC_X64 | 128 | 152 | 348 | `psx` | none | `st-v` |

**F-05 confirmed per device.** `st-v` has no emulator on H700, RK3326, RK3566 and GENERIC_X64, and `psx` has no default on GENERIC_X64 alone. `git diff upstream/next 8ebaf8daad -- virtual/emulators/package.mk` does not touch the `psx` or `st-v` blocks, so both are upstream's lines, carried into a fork-own file. The `psx` gap is the fork's because GENERIC_X64 is the fork's device. The `st-v` gap is a defect upstream ships too (#585 candidate, D-WORKFLOW-164).

### F-06 (Q3, Q7): the table's "only one default" rule is enforced by accident on one shape, silently, and not at all on the other

The README comment in `makeinstall_target` (`virtual/emulators/package.mk` 150 to 151) says: *"Only ONE core per system can be set as the default, setting multiple cores will result in a build failure."* No line checks this. What happens is decided by `add_es_system`'s default step (`functions` 212 to 214), `xmlstarlet ed --inplace -i "//system/emulators/emulator[@name=X]/cores/core" -t attr -n default -v true`. It adds the attribute to **every** core of that emulator added so far, not to the one just added. Two constructed cases, by the regeneration over a scratch copy of the recipe (`.qa-scratch/t3-regen/fake/`, GENERIC_X64):

- **Case A, two `true` lines in one emulator** (`3do`: `opera true`, then `opera_two true`): the first core gets the attribute twice (`<core default="true" default="true">opera</core>`), the pre-format file is malformed, and `mk_es_systems`'s `xmlstarlet fo` (`functions:113`) exits **2**. `set -e` ends the recipe there. **The build does fail, with no message**: `fo`'s stderr goes to `/dev/null`, and its reason (`Attribute default redefined`, read by running the same `fo` by hand) is never printed. The regeneration's log was empty; `bash -x` found the line.
- **Case B, a `false` core listed before the `true` one in the same emulator** (`bbcmicro`: `first_false false`, then `b2 true`): **both cores carry `default="true"`**, the file is well-formed, and the build succeeds. The table then holds two defaults, against the comment's rule, and which one the interface launches is the interface's choice.

**Today:** no device's table has two defaults (above), because every list puts its `true` line first in its emulator. So case B is latent, a trap for the next edit. Both cases sit in T5's `functions` (the slice reads it as context and the fix is T5's path), but the comment that misleads is this slice's recipe. Fix shape for the punch list: in `functions`, mark the core just added (`.../cores/core[last()]`) and make `mk_es_systems` print `fo`'s error. That is T5's, filed as a lead on #650 for the coordinator to route. On this slice's side, the comment should say what is checked (Q7).

### F-07 (Q4): upstream dropped `aethersx2-sa` on 2026-10-18 for a licensing concern; `next` still builds and ships it

`c4d353449e` (`upstream/next`, sunshineinabox, 2026-10-18, *"aethersx2: drop package due to licensing concern"*) removes the recipe, `licenses/AETHERSX2.pdf`, its per-device inis, `Start AetherSX2.sh`, its `es_features.cfg` block, its `rocknix-systems` and `factoryreset` lines, and 27 lines of `virtual/emulators/package.mk`. `next` still carries `aethersx2-sa` in `PKG_EMUS` for RK3399, RK3566/RK3576, RK3588, SM6115, SM4450, SM8250, **SM8550**, SM8650/SM8750 and S922X (`package.mk` 49 to 94), and the `ps2` arm installs `Start AetherSX2.sh`. **SM8550 is a device the fork builds**, so the fork's SM8550 image carries a package its upstream dropped over licensing. This is the first of Q4 (a)'s 14 dispositions, and its decision is not this lane's. The recipe is T30's (`emulators/standalone/aethersx2-sa`). The table lines and `Start AetherSX2.sh` are this slice's. The licence question belongs to #528's gates. **Posted on #650 for the coordinator, with D-UI-079 in view: it removes a player-visible emulator.** Disposition on the punch list: `to merge` pending that word, `Already written:` the PS2 settings and memory cards AetherSX2 left under `/storage` on SM8550 and RK3566 devices.

### F-08 (Q3, Q4): the default PlayStation core on H700, RK3326, RK3566 and SM8550 is not where the launcher loads it

**What the table says:** `virtual/emulators/package.mk` 1165 to 1189 makes `retroarch` / **`pcsx_rearmed32` the `default="true"` `psx` core** on H700|RK3326, RK3576|RK3566 and SM4450|SM8250|SM8550. That is four of the fork's five built devices, and the H700 and SM8550 images' tables carry it (`<core default="true">pcsx_rearmed32</core>`).

**What the launcher loads:** `runemu.sh` (T1's, read-only) switches `RABIN` to `retroarch32` for `pcsx_rearmed32`, `gpsp` and `desmume` on aarch64 (lines 268 to 279). It starts `/usr/bin/${RABIN} -L /tmp/cores/${CORE}_libretro.so` (302). `/tmp/cores` is RetroArch's `tmp-cores.mount`, an overlay whose only lower directory is **`/usr/lib/libretro`**, with `/storage/cores` above it (`emulators/libretro/retroarch/system.d/tmp-cores.mount`). `retroarch32bit-append.cfg`'s `libretro_directory = /usr/lib32/libretro` does not apply, because `-L` names an absolute path.

**What the images carry** (the M7 roots' install trees, built from `08cc7ff6cd`, whose slice bytes are `next`'s except `Install Steam.sh`):

| Image | `usr/lib/libretro/pcsx_rearmed32_libretro.so` | `usr/lib32/libretro/pcsx_rearmed32_libretro.so` | `gpsp`, `desmume` in `usr/lib/libretro` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| H700 (`m7-signin-h700-build-20261023-06`) | **absent** | present | present |
| SM8550 (`m7-signin-sm8550-build-20261023-03`) | **absent** | present | present |

`gpsp-lr` (24) and `desmume-lr` (34) each copy their 32-bit `.so` from `${ROOT}/build.${DISTRONAME}-${DEVICE}.arm/install_pkg/...` into `usr/lib/libretro`. **`pcsx_rearmed-lr` has no such copy**: its `makeinstall_target` installs `pcsx_rearmed32_libretro.so` only in the arm build, which `compat/lib32` then carries into `/usr/lib32`. No other writer puts a core under `/usr/lib/libretro` or `/storage/cores`: `git grep -E '/storage/cores|/tmp/cores'` finds only the mount and the `retroarch.cfg` keys.

**So, as the code and the images read, a PlayStation game launched with the default core on the fork's H700 and SM8550 images asks `retroarch32` to load a file that does not exist**, unless the player has put one in `/storage/cores` (the core updater's upper layer).

**Upstream found and fixed the same defect:** `c9820d0e89` (`upstream/next`, Jacob Cook, 2026-10-04, *"pcsx_rearmed-lr: install the 32-bit core where runemu loads it"*) adds exactly the missing copy. It is one of Q4 (a)'s 14, not on `next`.

**Can this be done on the VM?** No. The guest is x86_64, has no 32-bit RetroArch and no `pcsx_rearmed32` (`psx` there is F-05's). The load failure itself is a device fact. What an agent can verify on the host is the artifact: the file's presence in an arm-enabled image's `usr/lib/libretro`, read from the install tree or the `SYSTEM` squashfs. That is the criterion the bug carries. The verdict here rests on that artifact plus the code path, and the load failure is a lead until a device shows it. **Severity: High** (the default core of a common system on the fork's main handheld). It goes to a `bug` issue at stage D, and to #650 now because the M7 lane is testing an H700 image. **Tracked as #802**, filed by the coordinator on 2026-10-24 after verifying F-08 and F-09; the maintainer and the M7 lane were told, and no device run was asked. The fix comes with the base merge as `c9820d0e89` adapted to `${DISTRONAME}`; nothing on this branch. The punch item references #802 rather than filing a second bug.

`Already written:` nothing is written by the defect; a player who fetched `pcsx_rearmed32` into `/storage/cores` keeps that copy, which the overlay puts above the image's once the image carries one. That is read both ways, nothing to migrate.

### F-09 (Q4, interaction): the next base merge brings two copies that fail on the fork's build roots, and git merges them cleanly

`git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only next upstream/next` (read-only; result tree `d2df30a92a`): on the slice, exactly **five content conflicts**, all in the fork-own arcade recipes that carry the rotation-table hook (`fbalpha2012-lr`, `fbalpha2019-lr`, `fbneo-lr`, `mame2003-plus-lr`, `mame2010-lr`). Each is the fork's `PKG_DEPENDS_TARGET="toolchain Python3:host"` plus `PKG_NEED_UNPACK` against upstream's `af70bd435b` `PKG_DEPENDS_TARGET="toolchain retroarch"`; the resolution keeps both, `toolchain Python3:host retroarch`. The rest of the slice merges cleanly, including `virtual/emulators/package.mk` (the 29 conflicts tree-wide are outside this slice).

**But the clean part carries a trap.** The fork's ARM compatibility roots are `build.${DISTRONAME}-${DEVICE}.arm` (`43d0bc3bf4`, 2026-10-07; `DISTRONAME="pixelelated"`, `distributions/ROCKNIX/options:17`). Upstream's copies say `build.${DISTRO}-...`, and `DISTRO` is `ROCKNIX` here (`config/options:24`). `next` carries none (`git grep -F 'build.${DISTRO}-' next`: empty). The merged tree carries **two**, both new from upstream:

- `pcsx_rearmed-lr/package.mk:27`, the F-08 fix (`c9820d0e89`);
- `yabadabadoo-lr/package.mk:46`, the new core (`8704addb6b`).

On a fork build with `ENABLE_32BIT=true` both resolve to `build.ROCKNIX-<DEVICE>.arm`, which does not exist. Their `cp -a` fails, and `set -e` stops the build at that package. **So the fix for F-08 cannot be taken verbatim: the base merge must rewrite those two lines to `${DISTRONAME}`**, as `43d0bc3bf4` did for the other five. A check that would have caught it: `git grep -F 'build.${DISTRO}-'` empty on the merged tree.

### Q4 (c): the 21 RetroArch patches against master (03:18 UTC), `tools/q4c-patches.sh`

`q4c-patches.sh` fetches, from `libretro/RetroArch` through `gh api` (raw), the 25 files the series touches: 24 sources plus `retroarch/`-prefixed duplicates that `-p1` strips. It then applies the series in `scripts/unpack`'s order (`patches/*.patch` sorted, then `batteryplus/`, which the recipe adds through `PKG_PATCH_DIRS`). For each patch it records `git apply --check -p1` (#650 criterion 9's form) and `patch -p1 --fuzz=0 --dry-run`, then advances with `patch -p1` as `scripts/unpack:197` runs it (default fuzz). Outputs: `q4c-at-pin.txt`, `q4c-at-master-4fe09508d4.txt`. Master was **`4fe09508d4b9a2a1732e4249e7ee53af03ac68ed`** at 03:18:31; it had been `deebdf6a4c` at 02:58, and it moves by the hour.

| Patch | Author | At the pin (control): `git apply` / fuzz 0 / applied | At master: `git apply` / fuzz 0 / applied |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `0001-Increase-glui-size` | upstream | ok / ok / offset | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (2 of 2 hunks) |
| `0001-Increase-ozone-size` | upstream | ok / ok / offset | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (2 of 2) |
| `0001-Increase-ozone-widget_size` | upstream | ok / ok / offset | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (1 of 1) |
| `0002-quit-not-restart` | upstream | ok / ok / offset | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (1 of 1) |
| `0003-fix-oga-no-preferred` | upstream | ok / ok / clean | ok / ok / offset |
| `0004-drm-resolution` | upstream | **FAIL / FAIL / fuzz** | FAIL / FAIL / fuzz |
| `0006-no-screen-brightness` | upstream | ok / ok / offset | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (1 of 2) |
| `0008-disable_search` | upstream | ok / ok / offset | ok / ok / offset |
| `0009-hide-wayland-checkerboard` | upstream | ok / ok / offset | ok / ok / offset |
| `0010-paths` | upstream | ok / ok / clean | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (1 of 4) |
| `0011-cheevos-sounds` | upstream | ok / ok / offset | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (1 of 2) |
| `0011-video-thread-wrapper-serialise-posters` | **fork** | ok / ok / clean | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (4 of 4); **upstream's own form of it is on master** (#19518, merged 2026-09-08) |
| `0012-cheevos-cmd` | upstream | ok / ok / offset | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (1 of 1) |
| `0013-cheevos-offline-proxy-toast` | **fork** | ok / ok / clean | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (`intl/msg_hash_fr.h`, `msg_hash.h`) |
| `0014-widgets-backdrop-follows-the-font` | **fork** | ok / ok / clean | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (3 of 6) |
| `0015-video-thread-wrapper-run-a-command-once` | **fork** | ok / ok / clean | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (2 of 2); **its defect is already fixed on master** (#19577, its author: *"master already fixes this"*) |
| `0016-widgets-message-queue-floor` | **fork** | ok / ok / clean | FAIL / FAIL / **FAILED** (2 of 4) |

| `batteryplus/0001-batteryplus-capacity` | upstream | **FAIL** / ok / clean | FAIL / ok / offset |

**What it shows:**

- **The control holds.** The build's `patch -p1` applies all 21 at the pin, so the method reproduces the build.
- **One finding inside the control (F-10):** `0004-drm-resolution` applies to the pin only with fuzz, and `git apply` refuses it there.
- **Against master, a bump is a re-cut of the series, not a pin edit.** Fifteen patches fail outright, and two of the fork's (0011, 0015) are superseded by master's own fixes, so they are dropped, not rebased. Brief § 5 says the bump itself is a stream brief's decision; the punch list records it as `deferred:` with this table.
- A failure later in the series can be partly a consequence of an earlier one: several patches touch `gfx/gfx_widgets.c` and `configuration.c`. So each fork patch's master row is "does not apply to master as the build would meet it", not a hunk-exact rebase estimate.
- `git apply --check` refuses `batteryplus` at both refs while `patch` at fuzz 0 accepts it. The cause is not read here; the build uses `patch`, so it does not affect the build.

### F-10 (Q2): `0004-drm-resolution.patch` applies to the pin only with fuzz

At the pin, `patch -p1 --fuzz=0 --dry-run` refuses `0004-drm-resolution.patch` and `git apply --check` refuses it. The build's `patch -p1` (default fuzz 2) applies it, logging `with fuzz`. A patch that holds only by fuzz is the shape `150a4e1402` (2026-09-28, *"widget patches follow the drawn face and apply at fuzz 0"*) cured for the fork's widget patches: fuzz lets a patch land on the wrong lines when the context moves. Upstream-carried (ROCKNIX's patch); fix shape: refresh its context against the pin so it applies at fuzz 0, the same content. Proof: `q4c-patches.sh` at the pin reads `fuzz0=ok` for it. The build's own proof is an image, so `fix-pending-image`.

### `emulators/libretro/retroarch/package.mk` (153 lines, fork-own through `43d0bc3bf4`; read whole at `d606301784`)

History on the slice since the base: `43d0bc3bf4` (2026-10-07, the ARM compatibility roots renamed to `build.${DISTRONAME}`), plus two upstream merges. `upstream/next`'s copy differs only by that rename (F-09). Read line by line:

- **1 to 2, the header:** one line, `Copyright (C) 2024-present ROCKNIX`, the same as upstream's. No JELOS or LibreELEC line, and none is owed unless the recipe came from one. `git log --follow` reaches `9f1fab30f6` (2025-07-13, *"projects: add modifications for ROCKNIX"*, 14 commits in all) and no earlier. Q2 n/a.
- **10:** `libass` appears twice in `PKG_DEPENDS_TARGET`. Q7, cosmetic (upstream's).
- **17 to 24:** `arm) true` / `*) PKG_DEPENDS_TARGET+=" empty"`. `empty` is `projects/ROCKNIX/packages/tools/empty`, a placeholder dependency (upstream's idiom). Q7: a reader cannot tell why from the recipe.
- **26:** `batteryplus` only when `BATTERYPLUS_SUPPORT=yes` (Q4 (c) applies it last, as the build does).
- **66 to 76:** GLES only when both `OPENGLES_SUPPORT` and `PREFER_GLES` are `yes`, else full GL. GENERIC_X64 and SM8550 set `PREFER_GLES=no`, H700, RK3326 and RK3566 `yes` (their `options`).
- **92 to 98, Q1 (the brief's own lead):** three `make ...` followed by `[ $? -eq 0 ] && echo "(... ok)" || { echo "(... failed)" ; exit 1 ; }`. The build runs under `set -e` (`config/path:4`), so a failing `make` has already ended the function before the test: the test is dead in both branches. Fix shape: drop the three status lines (F-13).
- **106 to 114, F-11 below.**
- **117:** upstream's own `retroarch.cfg` goes to `/etc/retroarch.cfg`. `git grep -n -F '/etc/retroarch.cfg' -- projects packages distributions` (patches excluded) prints nothing, so no script of the tree reads it. RetroArch's own fallback reads it when started with no `--config`, and `runemu.sh` always passes one (302). So it is not reached on the launch path, and its one reader is a hand-started `retroarch`. Q6: kept as RetroArch's default reference; a row, no fix.
- **129 to 134:** a device with no `sources/<DEVICE>/` fails the build with `Configure retroarch for ${DEVICE}`, which is a guard that fails closed and says why. Q2 PASS.
- **137 to 138:** `.overlay` markers so the `tmp-*.mount` overlays have a lower directory. **147 to 153:** five mounts enabled (Q5: boot cost read with the units, group 1).

### F-11 (Q3): `retroarch32` is skipped silently whenever the ARM root's install directory is not exactly one

`package.mk` 106 to 112: on aarch64 the recipe installs `/usr/bin/retroarch32` only `if [ -f ${ROOT}/build.${DISTRONAME}-${DEVICE}.arm/install_pkg/retroarch-*/usr/bin/retroarch ]`. Two cases make that test false or an error (`[: too many arguments`), and the build then goes on without the 32-bit RetroArch and without saying so: no arm root at all (an aarch64 build run without its arm half), and two `retroarch-<pin>` install directories left in one root after a pin bump. Every 32-bit core (`pcsx_rearmed32`, `gpsp`, `desmume`, `runemu.sh` 268 to 279) then fails at launch, the same shape as F-08. **Today:** the glob also matches `retroarch-assets-*`, but the full path matches only the real binary. All four live arm roots on the host hold one `retroarch-bdba046fa6` install directory, and the H700 and SM8550 images carry `usr/bin/retroarch32` (14,159,572 and 14,683,872 bytes). So it is latent. Fix shape: on aarch64 with `ENABLE_32BIT=true`, require exactly one match and `exit 1` naming the path otherwise (the 129 to 134 guard's shape). Proof: a constructed arm root with zero and with two install directories, through the recipe's function in a sandbox (`fix-host-regen`'s method). Severity Medium (latent; its failure is silent and costs every 32-bit core).

### The per-device `retroarch.cfg` (fifteen sets; GENERIC_X64's fork-own, read whole for Q2, Q5, Q6, Q7)

**Keys the pinned RetroArch does not read (Q6, Q1).** The test, over every `.c`/`.h` of the pin's unpack in the M7 GENERIC_X64 root (`build/retroarch-bdba046fa6/`): a key is not read when it does not occur as a word anywhere in that source. Generated families are excluded (`input_*_btn|_axis|_mbtn`, `input_device_pN`, `input_libretro_device_pN`, `network_remote_enable_user_pN`, `input_playerN_*`), and a hotkey `input_<bind>` counts as read when `<bind>` occurs (RetroArch stringifies the bind names). The output is `q6-retroarch-cfg-keys-not-read.txt`. **GENERIC_X64: 24 of 855 keys; H700, RK3326, RK3566: 24 each; SM8550: 25; AMD64: 25.** GENERIC_X64's list:

- `check_firmware_before_loading`, `content_history_dir`, `content_show_add`, `cursor_directory`
- `emuelec_exit_to_kodi`, `video_oga_vertical_enable`: keys of the forks this tree descends from, never upstream RetroArch's
- `frame_time_counter_reset_after_fastforwarding`, `_after_load_state`, `_after_save_state`
- `input_duty_cycle`, `input_turbo_default_button`: the turbo keys, renamed
- `input_movie_record_toggle`, `input_send_debug_info`
- `menu_show_video_layout`, `video_layout_enable`, `video_layout_path`, `video_layout_selected_view`: the video-layout feature, retired
- `netplay_stateless_mode`, `playlist_cores`, `playlist_entry_remove`, `resampler_directory`
- `video_hdr_display_contrast`, `video_hdr_max_nits`, `video_scale_integer_overscale`

**This is a lower bound, not a list of every dead key.** `core_updater_buildbot_url` occurs as a word in the source and is still not read as a setting. `9fd73da845` (#307 PL-076) established that on 2026-09-28 by reading `configuration.c` 1683 to 1684 and dropped it from GENERIC_X64 alone. **The other fourteen profiles still carry it pointing at `.../linux/armhf/latest/`**, including the aarch64 H700, RK3326, RK3566 and SM8550 files (`grep core_updater_buildbot_url sources/*/retroarch.cfg`), which F-12 carries.

### F-12 (Q6, Q1): every shipped `retroarch.cfg` carries 24 or 25 keys the pinned RetroArch never reads, and the armhf core-updater URL on fourteen

The list above. RetroArch ignores an unknown key, so nothing breaks. What it costs:

- **A reader is misled about what is configured.** `content_history_dir` is blindspot 65's case: RetroArch 1.22 writes its history under `playlists/builtin/` (#306), and this key reads as if it set the place.
- **The renamed keys' values reach nothing.** The turbo pair and `video_hdr_*` probably have successor keys that sit at their defaults; whether the profiles' intent needs carrying to them is read key by key at fix time, from the pin's `configuration.c`.

**`content_history_dir` and `core_updater_buildbot_url` on GENERIC_X64 are the fork's lines**, and the rest are upstream's in all fifteen. Fix shape, the class #307 PL-076 set:

- remove the not-read keys from the GENERIC_X64 profile now;
- for the fourteen upstream profiles, remove them too, the same lines in every file, which D-UI-079 allows because nothing a player sees changes (RetroArch ignores them today);
- name each renamed key's successor and its current value in the commit.

Proof: the key test above reads 0 on the edited files, and a guest launch reads the same `retroarch.cfg` behaviour (`fix-guest-copy`). `Already written:` each device copies the profile to `/storage/.config/retroarch/retroarch.cfg` once (9fd73da845's own note), so existing devices keep the dead lines, harmlessly. Read both, nothing to migrate. Q1 / Q6, Low (`Renovation`).

**Q7, the fifteen files as copies:** compared by key and value (`python3 -I`, a dict per file), GENERIC_X64 against H700 differs in 89 keys (55 only in GENERIC_X64, 17 only in H700, 17 with different values). Against RK3326 and RK3566 it differs in 87; against SM8550 in 246. H700 against the other RK families differs in 20 to 33 lines; SM4450 equals SM8250 and SM8550 (0 changed lines) and differs from SM8650/SM8750 by 2. **So at least four of the fifteen are byte-for-byte copies of another** (SM4450, SM8250, SM8550, and SM8650 = SM8750 up to 2 lines), with no base file they derive from: a "diff against a base in disguise", the brief's Q7 question. Not changed here; a Q7 row for the coordinator (the files are upstream's, and folding them is a recipe change across devices the fork does not build).

### F-13 (Q1): RetroArch's `make_target` carries three status checks that `set -e` has made dead

`emulators/libretro/retroarch/package.mk` 92 to 98: each of the three `make` calls (RetroArch, `gfx/video_filters`, `libretro-common/audio/dsp_filters`) is followed by `[ $? -eq 0 ] && echo "(... ok)" || { echo "(... failed)" ; exit 1 ; }`. The recipe's functions run under `set -e` (`config/path:4`). A failing `make` therefore ends `make_target` before the next line, and a succeeding one prints a status the build log already carries. Fix shape: drop the three lines, behaviour unchanged. Proof: `pkgcheck retroarch` rc 0; the build log of the next image shows the three makes; `fix-pending-image`. Q1, Low (`Renovation`). Upstream's lines (#585 candidate).

### The eight fork-written RetroArch patches (1,244 lines; all seven questions)

**Q4 for all eight** is the table above: none applies to master as the build would meet it; 0011 and 0015 are superseded there. Read one by one:

- **`0011-video-thread-wrapper-serialise-posters`** (85 lines, `1c4fa3b308`, #211):
  - What it does: a `user_lock` serialises posters on the threaded wrapper's one-packet mailbox.
  - Q3: the header's argument holds in the code read at the pin: the video thread never takes `user_lock`, and every marshalled entry goes through `video_thread_send_and_wait_user_to_thread`.
  - Q6: superseded upstream by its author's own PR #19518 (merged 2026-09-08), so it **retires with a bump** and is kept until then.
  - Q5: one uncontended mutex per posted command. Not measured; it is not on the frame path.
  - Its mechanical check is not this tool: `tools/retroarch-wrapper-test` drives one poster, so it does not exercise 0011 (§ Mechanical checks).
- **`0015-video-thread-wrapper-run-a-command-once`** (89 lines, `e5d14d4220`, #211): the dequeue runs a posted command once. **Proven by `tools/retroarch-wrapper-test`**: 2000 of 2000 with it, 2011 of 2000 with it reversed. Q6: master already fixes it (#19577), so it retires with a bump.
- **`0013-cheevos-offline-proxy-toast`** (188 lines, `d5ea1bdedf`, #194):
  - What it does: reads `X-RA-Proxy: 1` / `X-RA-Offline: 1` from the response headers (`rcheevos_client_header_is_set`: case-insensitive name, blanks skipped, value exactly `1`, bounded by the header's NUL) and shows `RetroAchievements: Logged in as "%s" (offline).` (French beside it).
  - Q3: the two flags are reset at every HTTP callback, and the login callback reads them synchronously inside it, so they cannot belong to another request. A non-proxy server could send the header and change one word of a toast; that is not a security effect.
  - Q2: the strings in `msg_hash_us.h`/`msg_hash_fr.h`, the enum in `msg_hash.h`. PASS.
- **`0018-auto-slot-survives-content-load`** (181 lines, `8db9042afa`, `a8175c6193`, `c2bdcf0797`, `f53264dcb0`, `a81db9971b`; #209, #249; D-LAUNCH-005, D-LAUNCH-007):
  - What it does: keeps `state_slot = -1` (Auto) when this launch's append config sets it, in both `command_event_set_savestate_auto_index` and `runtime_log_init`'s restore.
  - Q3: the detection is `config_get_entry(conf, "state_slot") != main_slot` after the append files load. It is sound only if loading an append file that sets the key points the map at a new entry. **The pinned `libretro-common/file/config_file.c` `config_append_file` (761 to 793) does exactly that** (`RHMAP_SET_FULL(conf->entries_map, new_hash, new_key, entry)` with the appended file's own entry), so reading supports it.
  - **The patch's own header says this final form "has not yet run on a guest"**. The guest proof below is the answer (D-LAUNCH-007's case).
  - Q4: #19223 (merged after the pin) rewrote the runtime log's slot handling, so 0018's second half needs re-reading at a bump.
- **`0017-widgets-sharp-font-sizes`** (230 lines, `eb131d112f`, `24badef3cb`, `150a4e1402`; #255, D-UI-0xx): moves a computed widget size up to a "sharp" one from a table measured for **Inter** (`ozone/regular.ttf`, `ozone/bold.ttf`), chosen by the file the face is loaded from. F-14 below.

### F-14 (Q6): patch 0017's size step engages on no shipped profile

0017's table is chosen by the face's file. Its lookup (`gfx_widgets_sharp_face`, patch lines 107 to 119) matches only `ozone/regular.ttf` and `ozone/bold.ttf` (lines 88 to 89), and a NULL or empty `video_font_path` selects the ozone faces. **All fifteen shipped profiles set `video_font_path = "/usr/share/retroarch-assets/xmb/monochrome/font.ttf"`** (`grep -H '^video_font_path' sources/*/retroarch.cfg`, fifteen identical lines), and neither `runemu.sh` nor `setsettings.sh` writes `video_font_path` (`grep -n video_font`, no hit). The patch's own header says so: *"ROCKNIX's shipped profiles name xmb/monochrome/font.ttf in video_font_path, which is M+ 1p. It has no table ... the faces those profiles draw keep their computed size."* **So on the shipped configuration 0017 changes no size, and what runs of it is the per-face log line** (`no sharp-size table for this face`), to be read on the guest (below). It is reached only when a player sets `video_font_path` to an ozone face by hand. Not a defect: a mechanism carried for a configuration nothing ships. **Q6/Q7, Low (`Renovation`); its disposition is the coordinator's.** The candidates: keep it, with the recipe or the profile saying it is for a configuration the fork may choose; ship an Inter `video_font_path` (a player-visible change, D-UI-079, the maintainer's); or drop it at the bump where it must be re-cut anyway (Q4 (c)). Also `deferred:#336` for the 2D cores (the widget surface retires there). `Already written:` nothing; a log line.

## Guest proofs

The guest: `/workspace/artifacts/m6-t3-20261024/t3.qcow2`, converted from the copied image (`sha256sum -c` OK) and resized to 16 GiB. It was started at 03:26:02 UTC with `generic-x64-vm run --headless --daemonize --pidfile .../qemu.pid --serial .../serial.sock --monitor .../monitor.sock --vnc 21 --ssh-port 10034` (QEMU pid 304271; KVM, Haswell-v4, 4 vCPUs, 8 GiB; virgl on the Intel iGPU). No other QEMU was running. On the second boot (uptime 15.7 s) the guest's `/etc/os-release` read **`BUILD_ID="069fc270442134fe771258418adf4e666e0473fb"`**, `VERSION="0.0.1-dev+20261022T235948Z.g069fc2704421"`, the `.identity.json`'s. The lane's own key is under the artifacts directory.

**Can this be done on the VM?** Yes for both proofs below: the image's installed set and RetroArch's start on x86_64. No for F-08's load failure (no 32-bit RetroArch on x86_64) and for any device's own `retroarch.cfg` on its panel (brief § 5).

### P1, the installed set on the running guest (03:27 UTC; `p1-installed-set.out` in the artifacts directory)

**proved: image-set.**

### F-15 (Q3, Q4): `melonds-ds-lr` fetches eleven dependencies from the network at configure time, by short hash or tag

`cmake/FetchDependencies.cmake` (the core's own, at its pin `bc4e4b67d2`) calls `fetch_dependency` for:

- melonDS `7117178`, libretro-common `ad9124f`, embed-binaries `078b62b`, glm `e7970a8`, libslirp `e61dbd4`, pntr `922aed0`;
- fmt **`11.2.0`** (a tag), yamc `4e015a7`, span-lite `00afc28`, date `1ead671`, zlib **`v1.3.1`** (a tag);
- tracy `v0.11.1` (off).

Each is a `FetchContent_Declare(GIT_REPOSITORY ... GIT_TAG ...)`, cloned during the build's configure into `.x86_64-rocknix-linux-gnu/_deps/<name>-src` (all eleven present in the M7 GENERIC_X64 root; `melonds` HEAD `7117178c2d…`, `fmt` `40626af88b…`, `zlib` `51b7f2abda…`, `libslirp` `e61dbd459c…`). The recipe sets no `FETCHCONTENT_FULLY_DISCONNECTED` and no `FETCHCONTENT_SOURCE_DIR_<NAME>` (`melonds-ds-lr/package.mk` 14 to 21 pass only the GL options).

**So this core's image is built from code no recipe in this tree pins with a full hash**, against a network at configure time. That is blindspot 47's shape (#226, pango's cairo) through cmake, which has no `--wrap-mode=nodownload` switch. Concretely:

- a build container without DNS fails here;
- a moved tag (`fmt`, `zlib`) changes the image silently;
- the core links its own `zlib` 1.3.1 beside the image's.

`melonds-ds-lr` is in `LIBRETRO_CORES` for every device, so it ships on all five of the fork's images, and it is the `nds` core beside `melonds`/`desmume`. Upstream-carried recipe; upstream builds the same way (CI has network).

Fix shape for the coordinator's stream brief: carry the dependencies as pinned sources, either `PKG_URL`-style extra tarballs with `PKG_SHA256`, or per-dependency packages wired through `FETCHCONTENT_SOURCE_DIR_<NAME>`, and set `-DFETCHCONTENT_FULLY_DISCONNECTED=ON`, so a missing one fails the configure by name. Proof: a build in a container with no network configures (`fix-pending-image`), and the `_deps` find reads empty for the core. Severity Medium. `Already written:` nothing on devices; this is a build-reproducibility defect. #585 candidate (upstream's recipe).

### F-16 (Q2): `easyrpg-lr/wildmidi` pins a short hash

`easyrpg-lr/wildmidi/package.mk:22` sets `PKG_VERSION="405ca73"` with `PKG_URL="${PKG_SITE}/archive/${PKG_VERSION}.tar.gz"`. `CLAUDE.md` and `packaging-and-patches.md` say a git source is pinned with the **full** hash (an abbreviated one stops resolving once the object count grows). `gh api repos/Mindwerks/wildmidi/commits/405ca73` resolves it today to `405ca73adfa11659b7579f0b09df1303f42659a4` (2018-11-24); `upstream/next` pins the same short form. Fix shape: the full hash; the tarball's bytes and `PKG_SHA256` are unchanged (GitHub archives a commit, not its name). Proof: `pkgcheck wildmidi` rc 0, then `scripts/get` fetching the same `PKG_SHA256` at the next build (`fix-pending-image`). Q2, Low; #585 candidate. The other six non-full pins are not this defect: `libxmp-lite` `4.5.0` is a release tarball, `freej2me-lr` `1.52` a tag tarball, `idtech-lr` `1.0` a label over a full-hash URL, and the three `vitaquake2-*` take `vitaquake2-lr`'s full hash.

### F-17 (Q7): the rotation-table hook is written out five times

The same 18 lines, the dependency, `PKG_NEED_UNPACK`, a ten-line comment and the generator's call, are in all five arcade recipes, differing only in the generator's name, the drivers directory and the output file. A reader meets the same reasoning five times, and an edit to the hook is five edits (the base merge's five conflicts, F-09, are this copy seen from the other side). Fix shape: none that the build engine offers cleanly (recipes share no include mechanism but `. ${ROOT}/packages/...`, which the stamp trap of `device-builds.md` § After rebasing, item 3 makes costly). So it is recorded with the reason the copies stay. Q7, Low (`Renovation`), withdraw-able at Phase 7 with that reason.

### F-18 (Q6, Q7): `X64_SKIP_CORES` says its cores are "tracked in issue #16", which is closed and tracked nothing of the kind

`virtual/emulators/package.mk` 107 to 115: *"GENERIC_X64 (x86_64): drop libretro cores that don't yet build for x86_64. These are tracked in issue #16 to fix/re-enable per-core later (e.g. parallel-n64-lr's x86 hacktarux dynarec defines fsqrt(), which collides with glibc's C23 fsqrt)."*

- **#16 was closed on 2026-09-24** (*"Closed as delivered in the 2026-09-24 triage (#262, bucket A): GENERIC_X64 landed ... The bare-metal x86_64 question is #230's."*). It was the VM build's issue, not a per-core list.
- The four skipped cores (`parallel-n64-lr`, `flycast2021-lr`, `duckstation-lr`, `idtech-lr`; `b3bf4ac176`, `a68b4056c3`, `d4fbdbf615` of 2026-08-21) are therefore tracked nowhere. (F-05's missing `psx` default on the VM is not one of their effects: the `psx` `case` has no GENERIC_X64 arm at all, so with the skip gone the table would still carry no default.)
- Fix shape: the comment names a live issue that holds the four, with each core's reason (the coordinator files or names it), or says the skip is permanent. Q6/Q7, Low.

### `virtual/emulators/sources/Install Steam.sh` (2,755 lines, fork-own; every item here is `deferred:M7-merge`)

**How it was read.** A read-only research subagent read the whole file and returned leads with line numbers (lead, not evidence, SKILL.md § Evidence floor 5). Each lead below was re-read at its lines in `next`'s copy (`8ebaf8daad`) before it was written; leads not re-read are marked as leads.

**Structure (Q7).**
- `#!/bin/bash`, `set -e` (6), `set -o pipefail` (7), no `set -u`, no `trap` (`grep -c -E '^\s*trap '` 0).
- Shell functions around five embedded Python 3 programs, run `-I -S -B`:
  - `steam_root_layout` (19 to 201)
  - `ensure_fex_rootfs` (249 to 762)
  - `install_steam_runtime_arm64` (775 to 1155)
  - `steam_client_state` (1167 to 2365, about 1,200 lines)
  - `install_proton_variant` (2372 to 2672)
- The main sequence is 2712 to 2755.
- **Installed where:** `steam` is in `PKG_EMUS` on SM4450, SM8250, **SM8550**, SM8650 and SM8750 (`package.mk` 72 to 89), so on one of the fork's built devices. `install_script "Install Steam.sh"` sits at 758. Q6 Table 3 lists it for SM8550 alone among the five.
- M7's `6101242e14` changes eight hunks (§ Scope's table); the items below say where that commit touches them.

### F-19 (Q3): on the SM8550 image, Install Steam stops at its Proton step, because that image's `grep` has no `-P`, and whether it does is decided by a build race

**The script:** `fetch_proton_latest_release` (203 to 247) parses GitHub's release JSON with `grep -oP '"tag_name":\s*"\K[^"]+'` (217) and `grep -oP '"browser_download_url":...'` (222), each `|| die "Failed to parse ..."` under `set -o pipefail`. The main sequence calls it twice, after the FEX rootfs, the runtime and the client are installed and before both Protons and the first launch (2747 to 2753). After `source /etc/profile` `PATH` is `/usr/bin:/usr/sbin`, so `/usr/bin/grep` (GNU grep 3.9) runs; busybox's has no `-P` either.

**The images** (the M7 roots' install trees):

| Image | `usr/bin/grep` | PCRE in it | The grep build's `config.h` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GENERIC_X64 | 880,192 bytes | yes (`exhausted PCRE JIT stack`, static) | `#define HAVE_LIBPCRE 1` |
| H700 | 789,952 bytes | yes | `#define HAVE_LIBPCRE 1` |
| **SM8550** | **265,312 bytes** | **no**: it carries `Perl matching not supported in a --disable-perl-regexp build`, and links only `libc.so.6` | **no `HAVE_LIBPCRE`** |

**The cause, a race:**

- `sysutils/grep/package.mk` declares `PKG_DEPENDS_TARGET="toolchain"` and passes no PCRE option, so grep's configure autodetects PCRE2 in the sysroot.
- On the SM8550 root its `config.log` reads `checking for pcre2_compile` then `conftest.c:457:38: fatal error: pcre2.h: No such file or directory`. PCRE2's `build_target` stamp is 09:46:50 and grep's 09:47:08, so grep configured while PCRE2 was still being built and installed into the sysroot by the parallel build.
- On the H700 root PCRE2 finished at 06:57:29, a minute before grep (06:58:29), and the same check reads `result: yes`.

**So, as the artifacts read, on the fork's SM8550 image Install Steam installs FEX, the runtime and the client, then dies with `Failed to parse Proton-CachyOS version tag.`**, the parse failing before the network answer is read.

- The `grep -oP` is upstream's (`ff40ff1897`, 2026-04-28), still in M7's copy (`069fc2704421` lines 223 and 228); `6101242e14` does not touch it.
- **Sibling:** `projects/ROCKNIX/packages/hardware/quirks/platforms/RK3566/bin/volumealign:18` uses `grep -ozP` and is exposed to the same race on RK3566 images (another slice's file).

**Can this be done on the VM?** The mechanism, yes in part: the GENERIC_X64 grep happens to have PCRE, so the guest cannot show the failure with the image's own grep. A guest run of `fetch_proton_latest_release` against a fixture JSON with a no-PCRE grep shim on `PATH` would show the parse dying (a substitute input, named as such, P-05). The criterion that needs no device is the artifact: the image's `grep -P` support, read from the image, or the script not using `-P` (`git grep`).

Fix shapes, two layers:
- in this slice, parse the JSON with the `python3 -I` the script already runs (`json.loads`), removing the dependency on `grep -P`;
- in the grep recipe (another slice, routed through the coordinator), `PKG_DEPENDS_TARGET+=" pcre2"` and `--enable-perl-regexp`, so a build without it fails rather than drops the feature.

**Severity High** (a Tools entry that cannot complete on a built device). **Tracked as #806** (the coordinator, 2026-10-24): the recipe layer is T22's (#669), and this slice's script layer stays `deferred:M7-merge` referencing #806. `Already written:` a failed run leaves FEX, the runtime and the client installed under `/storage`, and a later successful run reuses them through the script's own state checks (`steam_client_state`'s "existing" branch, 2292 to 2294). Read both, nothing to migrate.

### F-20 (Q2, security): network data reaches `eval`

240 to 243: `eval "${var_prefix}_VERSION_FULL='${version_clean}'"`, and the same for `_TAR`, `_DIR` and `_URL`, insert values parsed from GitHub's release JSON inside single quotes. The extraction regexes exclude `"` but not `'` (217, 222), so a `'` in a tag name or an asset URL would close the quote and run what follows as shell. Python's checks of the name and URL (2584 to 2588) run after the `eval`. The attacker is whoever controls those releases' metadata, behind HTTPS. **M7's `6101242e14` replaces exactly these four `eval`s** with literal assignment and limits `var_prefix` to its two callers (its first two hunks), so the fix exists and arrives with the M7 candidate's merge. Severity Medium; `deferred:M7-merge`, resolved by that commit when it lands. `*_VERSION_FULL` is also never read (only its assignment, Q6).

### F-21 (Q3, security): the Steam runtime and Proton archives are installed without an integrity check

- **The runtime:** the version string comes from `curl -fsSL` (1060 to 1062, its shape checked by a regex), and the archive from `wget -t 3 -T 30 -O <work>/runtime.tar.xz` (1069 to 1071). Only the archive's structure and ELF headers are checked afterwards (lead lines 1080 to 1101, 856 to 875).
- **Proton:** `wget` at 2630, then only `0 < size <= MAX_ARCHIVE` (2632) and the manifest shape. The release's `.sha512sum` asset is deliberately skipped (222's `grep -v '\.sha512sum'`) and never fetched.
- **The client, by contrast,** is checked against the manifest's `sha2` and size (1749 to 1751).

So two of the three downloaded payloads that the script then runs are trusted on TLS alone. Lead, not re-read: neither `curl` call nor `wget` restricts redirects to HTTPS (the client transfer does, `--proto =https --proto-redir =https`, around 1833 to 1872), and `wget` is probably busybox's (`-t` may be ignored). Fix shape: fetch and check Proton's `.sha512sum`. For the runtime, use the checksum file Valve publishes beside its images, if one exists, which is a lead to read. Severity Medium; `deferred:M7-merge`.

### F-22 (Q3): an interrupted first launch leaves binfmt off

`run_steam_first_launch` (2692 to 2710) writes `0` to `/proc/sys/fs/binfmt_misc/{x86,box32,box64}` (2694 to 2696) and restores them only by `systemctl restart systemd-binfmt` at its end (2709). There is no `trap`. An interrupt or a kill while Steam runs (2703; the player closing the terminal) leaves x86/box64 binfmt disabled until a reboot or a restart of that unit, which breaks every box64 or FEX launch meanwhile. Fix shape: `trap 'systemctl restart systemd-binfmt' EXIT INT TERM` around the block. Severity Low; `deferred:M7-merge`.

### F-23 (Q6, Q7): dead assignments, comments that disagree, and logic written several times

- **Read as dead:** `FEX_ARCH_USR_LIB` (2723), `RUNTIME_DIR` (2724), `STEAM_MANIFEST_URL` (2728) and `STEAM_CDN` (2729) each occur once (`grep -c -w`), their assignment; the embedded Python reads `sys.argv`, not the environment, and has these values as literals of its own. `*_VERSION_FULL` (F-20) is the same. shellcheck flagged the four (SC2034).
- **Comments that disagree (leads):** `install_bundled_proton_files` logs "Installing bundled Proton files..." and copies only `registry.vdf` (2368 to 2369); 2374 calls the fifth argument "retained for caller compatibility" while `FAMILIES[family]` reads it (2583); `ensure_steam_desktop_stub` is an empty `touch` (769 to 772).
- **Copies (leads):** a mountinfo octal-unescape parser six times; `no_mounts` three times; two VDF parsers with different grammars (1719, 2425).
- **Q5 lead:** `ensure_fex_rootfs` runs `begin`, `stage` and `activate` on every run (727 to 731, re-read). Whether `stage` downloads a whole ArchLinux rootfs again when a valid one is present is the subagent's lead (lines 499 to 501), not re-read.

Severity Low (`Renovation`); all `deferred:M7-merge`.

**The three remaining widget patches** (#194, #251, #255, #295, #296; D-UI-0xx; their Q1, Q5 and Q7 are `deferred:#336` for the 2D cores, whose widget surface Step 1 retires, and Q2/Q3 stand regardless):

- **`0014-widgets-backdrop-follows-the-font`** (248 lines, `5eef6ee32d`): re-measures a notification's width with the font it is drawn with, after the ozone 1.5 multiplier that `0001-Increase-ozone-widget_size` applies only in `gfx_widgets_iterate`. Q3: the header's numbers (9 px against 10 px, 199 against 226 px) are the case it fixes. It applies at fuzz 0 at the pin (Q4 (c)). PASS, reading.
- **`0016-widgets-message-queue-floor`** (100 lines, `443028ff7a`, `7fd4864597`): `gfx_widgets_font_init` takes a floor, 9 for regular and bold and 13 for the message queue. **Seen engaging on the guest** (P3): `font msg_queue: ... computed 8.22 px, floor 13, ... drawn 13 px`. Its justification (Inter's stems at 13 px) was measured for Inter. The shipped profiles draw M+ 1p (F-14), where `0017`'s own header measures 29 % lit columns at 13 px, so the floor makes the queue bigger, not sharper, on the shipped face. That is a Q7 note riding with F-14, not a defect.
- **`0019-widgets-message-queue-keeps-its-place`** (123 lines, eight cuts `0e0ffbe3cf` to `9a64a4ad8f`, #295/#296): the queue's placement follows the drawn box, and its foot is the left margin (`msg_queue_bottom_margin = msg_queue_rect_start_x`). Q3: `gfx_widgets_layout` now calls `gfx_widgets_msg_queue_move` when messages are queued (pinned `gfx_widgets.c:1219 to 1220`, after layout's own unlock at 1196). `msg_queue_move` takes `current_msgs_lock` itself (676, 728), so the call is neither unlocked nor recursive. The unlocked read of `current_msgs_size` decides only whether to call. PASS, reading. Q4: at master it applies only with fuzz.

## Coverage Boundary

**Examined:**
- **Read whole:** RetroArch's `package.mk` and the eight fork patches. `virtual/emulators/package.mk` through its regeneration, the GENERIC_X64 arms and the fork's 94-line delta; the GENERIC_X64 profile through its key analysis and Q5 keys; the fork's core recipes and the two generators.
- **Read through leads:** `Install Steam.sh` (a subagent's leads, each re-read at its lines).
- **The upstream-carried recipes and patches:** at Q4 and Q6.
- **Runtime-probed on the guest:** the installed set, time to play, 0017, 0018, the logging key.
- **Host-reproduced:** the table (byte-identical on three images) and the rotation tables (byte-identical).

**Deliberately not examined:** the 57 core patches (T42's) and the five T24 recipes beyond Q6. The standalone emulators (T30's). `distributions/ROCKNIX/config/functions` beyond the table's machinery (T5's). The 14 upstream device profiles' values on their own panels (no guest has them). `Install Steam.sh` line by line by this orchestrator (its findings are `deferred:M7-merge`, and the file changes by 605 lines in the M7 merge).

**Dimensions not exercised:**
- a 32-bit RetroArch (#802's load failure);
- an arm build of any recipe;
- a device;
- the exit hotkey end to end (T1's);
- the load-state hotkey after 0018 (#249).

### F-24 (Q3): on SM8550 the exit hotkey's kill target for RPCS3 names a process that never runs

`virtual/emulators/sources/Start RPCS3.sh:27` registers `set_kill set "-9 rpcs3"`, and line 29 starts `/usr/bin/rpcs3-sa`. The SM8550 image carries the binary as `usr/bin/rpcs3-sa` and no `rpcs3` (`ls` of the install tree). The exit hotkey kills the registered name (`set_kill`, T1's `001-functions`), so on SM8550 it targets a name no process has. Upstream's `eeb33c5a37` (2026-10-05, *"emulators: fix quit hotkey targets for RPCS3, AetherSX2 and mupen64plus"*) changes it to `rpcs3-sa`; it applies to `next` cleanly (Q4 (a) row 9). Whether the hotkey path then also matches by a pattern that would catch `rpcs3-sa` anyway is T1's `input_sense` reading, a lead not settled here. **Can this be done on the VM?** No: GENERIC_X64 carries no RPCS3. The artifact (the binary's name against the registered target) is host-readable. Severity Medium; `to merge` with the base merge (`eeb33c5a37`).

## 03-retrospective.md (whole)

# Retrospective Audit — M6.P2 T3, RetroArch and the libretro cores

**Auditor:** Code Auditor skill (v1.13.0), primary Claude Opus 5.5 at high effort
**Date:** 2026-10-24
**Subject:** #650 (`M6.P2 E798/T3`), read at `reviewed-at: d606301784` (the slice's bytes equal to `8ebaf8daad`)
**Spec:** `docs/reviews/briefs/emulators-libretro.md` version 2; #650

---

## Running Notes

### 3.1 Architectural coherence

The slice is three mechanisms that meet at one file, `/usr/bin/runemu.sh`:

1. **What the image carries.** The core recipes, chosen per device by the metapackage's lists, with RetroArch and its 21 patches beside them. The lists are assembled from three variables (`LIBRETRO_CORES`, `EMUS_32BIT`, `PKG_EMUS`), an eleven-arm device `case`, an aarch64 addition and a GENERIC_X64 subtraction (`X64_SKIP_CORES`). That is coherent but opaque: what a device carries is a computation, and Q6 Table 1 is the first place it is written out.
2. **What the interface offers.** The launch table, 583 `add_emu_core` and 142 `add_es_system` calls in one 1,630-line function. It is produced by T5's `functions` through `xmlstarlet`, and the regeneration proves it is the build's own (byte-identical on three images).
3. **How RetroArch starts.** The per-device profile, the core options and the patches, composed at run time by T1's `setsettings.sh`.

The incoherence is at the joints, where each mechanism assumes the others agree:

- the table names a core the image may not carry where the launcher loads it (#802);
- the table may carry a system with no emulator (`st-v` on four built devices) or no default (`psx` on the VM);
- the profile carries keys RetroArch no longer reads (F-12).

### 3.3 Spec fidelity

The brief's claims re-derived, with the deltas recorded:

- **No delta** on the 344 / 31,961 / tier counts (`01` § Scope).
- **Delta:** the table's `500 <core` elements are 348 `<core>` plus 152 `<cores>`.
- **Delta:** the brief's 131 `*-lr` recipes in the membership are 132 by suffix (`neocd_lr`).
- **Correction:** `6101242e14` is not on `origin`; `origin/feature/m7-player-setup` is ahead only by `d9054efc46`, whose `Install Steam.sh` equals `next`'s.
- **Gap:** the host regeneration needs the build's host `xmlstarlet` for every core, not only for formatting; it was closed with the build root's own copy.
- **Delta:** the H700 root is `m7-signin-h700-build-20261023-06`, not `-05`.
- **Wording:** #650's criterion 9 asks `git apply --check` where the brief § 4 says `patch --dry-run`; both were run.

### 3.4 Platform architecture conformance

The fork's five built devices against upstream's fifteen profiles:

- **Fork devices with an upstream-shaped profile:** H700, RK3326, RK3566, SM8550.
- **The fork's own:** GENERIC_X64.
- **Kept for upstream:** the other ten profiles (Table 4).

The VM image is a faithful stand-in for the installed set (P1), and not for the 32-bit path (no `retroarch32` on x86_64), which is exactly where #802 sits.

### 3.5 Cross-system interaction audit

### Interaction: the launch table × `runemu.sh` (T1's #648; #730 open)

- **`-P%SYSTEM%` after `%ROM%`:** `RUNCMD` places `-P%SYSTEM%` directly after `%ROM%`, and `runemu.sh` reads the system as `${ARGUMENTS##*-P}`, the last `-P` on the line. A ROM path holding `-P` reaches #730's territory. The table side is fixed text (`functions:128`), and T1's #730 owns the parse. Lead only, not changed here.
- **The save-state flags after `--controllers=`:** these are a contract between the interface and the launcher that the table does not state (the table's template ends at `--controllers="%CONTROLLERSCONFIG%"`; the interface appends the words).
- **Seen engaging (P3b):** the flags are read only as separate words. P3's first launch, which packed them inside the value, showed what a third writer of that line would get wrong.
- **#802:** the table names `pcsx_rearmed32`, and the launcher loads it through `/tmp/cores` over `/usr/lib/libretro` (P1 shows the overlay on the guest), where the image does not put it.

### Interaction: the launch table × `SystemData` (T2's #649)

Read at the interface's pin `16dbac5211` (`SystemData.cpp` 2179 to 2213):

- **No default** (F-05, `psx` on GENERIC_X64): `getDefaultEmulator` falls back to the first emulator, so on the VM `psx` launches **DuckStation standalone** (`duckstation-sa`, first in the table) without a word. It is a VM-only behaviour, but it is the VM every change is QA'd on first.
- **No emulator** (F-05, `st-v` on H700, RK3326, RK3566 and the VM): `getDefaultEmulator` returns `""`, so a launch has no command to build.
- **Two defaults** (F-06's case B, latent): `getDefaultCore` returns the first core marked default in table order, which is **the core listed `false`**. The latent defect would launch the wrong core.
- **The overlays** (`es_systems_custom.cfg`, `es_systems_*.cfg`) are read before the table, and one unparseable overlay skips every later one (T2's SD-3, reproduced there as G-5). The table this slice generates is the base the overlays modify, so its correctness is the floor under them.
- **The tokens:** the table emits five tokens; the interface also expands `%BASENAME%`, `%ROM_RAW%`, `%GAMENAME%`, `%SYSTEMNAME%` and `%GAMEINFOXML%`, raw (T2's FD-3). No shipped system emits them (`grep -c` over the five regenerated tables: 0), so they are reachable only by a player's own `es_systems_custom.cfg`.

### Interaction: the per-device `retroarch.cfg` × `setsettings.sh` (T1's)

- **Copied once, then layered:** the profile is copied once to `/storage/.config/retroarch/retroarch.cfg` (9fd73da845's note), then `setsettings.sh` writes its keys into the append config for every launch (`Added setting: state_slot = "-1"` in P3b).
- **Which keys it manages:** of the Q5 keys, `setsettings.sh` manages `video_shader_enable`, `rewind_enable`, `run_ahead_enabled`, `audio_latency`, `cheevos_*`, `netplay_*`, `menu_driver` and `savestate_auto_*` (grep counts in `04` § Q5). The profile's values for those are the defaults the per-game settings override.
- **Which it does not:** `log_verbosity`, `video_threaded`, `video_font_path` and `content_history_size` are not managed, so the profile alone decides them. That is why F-14 (the font) and P4 (the logging) are the profile's.
- **Upgrade consequence:** an upgraded device keeps its first copy of the profile, so a profile fix (F-12) reaches only fresh installs and keys `setsettings.sh` rewrites. Each such fix carries that line as its `Already written:`.

### Interaction: the cores' saves × the cloud sync's layout

- **Core pins:** the metapackage's `core-pins` (byte-identical to the images' on three devices) is what the capture step records as a save's `core_build` (#21, D-CLOUD-017).
- **A core bump moves that pin:** Q4 (a) row 14 (`np2kai-lr`) and Q4 (b)'s 102 `behind-project` rows are each a possible state-format change. Every `to merge` of a core bump cites #10, and the reading of its range is the stream's.
- **#802's fix and the save layout:** #802's fix adds a file under `/usr/lib/libretro` and changes no save path. Whether a state written by the 32-bit `pcsx_rearmed32` loads in the 64-bit `pcsx_rearmed`, which a player using the workaround meanwhile would rely on, is #10's question, unchanged by #802 and not read here.

### Interaction: the widget and cheevos patches × the proxy (#163)

- **0013 depends on the proxy:** 0013 reads `X-RA-Proxy` and `X-RA-Offline`, which the proxy's own patch 011 writes, so the toast's correctness rests on the proxy writing them. That is a cross-repository contract with no test in this tree; a lead for the offline-achievements area.
- **The widgets draw the proxy's banners:** 0014, 0016 and 0019 draw the toasts the proxy causes. On the shipped profile they draw M+ 1p (F-14), not the Inter that 0016 and 0017 were measured on.

### 3.6 What's missing?

- **A check that the table's cores exist where the launcher loads them.** #802 is a table-versus-image mismatch that no check catches: the regeneration proves the table is the build's, and nothing proves the build put each named core at `/usr/lib/libretro/<core>_libretro.so`. A host check over an install tree (every `<core>` of `es_systems.cfg` against the `.so` files, with the known non-libretro emulators excluded) would have caught it. It is a tool recommendation for `04`.
- **A check that a build's optional features are what they were last time.** grep's PCRE (#806) changed between two images of one source with no message. A list of `config.h` feature defines, or binary feature strings, compared across device roots is the class of check.
- **A full-hash rule and a no-fetch rule for cmake cores** (F-15, F-16). `pkgcheck` checks neither.
- **`tools/retroarch-wrapper-test` covers 0015 only:** 0011's poster lock has no driving case, and `--gated` asserts nothing (F-01).

| A `grep -P` consumer | two (`Install Steam.sh`; RK3566's `volumealign`) |

### 3.7 Retrospective Summary

## Retrospective Summary

### Architectural Assessment

Coherent within each mechanism, fragile at the joints (§ 3.1). The table is proven to be the build's own, and the images to be the tree's, so every finding is a finding about what ships.

### Cornerstone Alignment

Good on the fork's own lines (the rotation hooks, `idtech-lr`'s pinned data, the patches' headers). Weaker where upstream's lines met the fork's changes: F-09, #806, F-15.

### Cross-System Interactions

Five seams. Two carry live defects:

- the table against the image, #802;
- the build order against a script's tool, #806.

One carries latent defects: the table against `SystemData` (no default, no emulator, two defaults).

### Spec Drift

Seven brief deltas, recorded in § 3.3, none a stop.

### Missing Artifacts

These are recommendations, not this slice's to build (`04` § Instruction File Recommendations):

- a table-against-image core check;
- a build-feature drift check;
- a no-fetch guard for cmake cores.

## 04-analysis.md (through the Instruction File Recommendations; the Second opinion section is this call)

# Analysis — M6.P2 T3, RetroArch and the libretro cores

**Auditor:** Code Auditor skill (v1.13.0), primary Claude Opus 5.5 (`anthropic/claude-opus-5-5`) at high effort
**Date:** 2026-10-24
**Subject:** #650 (`M6.P2 E798/T3`), slice `emulators-libretro`, 344 paths; `reviewed-at: d606301784`, the slice's bytes equal to `8ebaf8daad`
**Spec:** `docs/reviews/briefs/emulators-libretro.md` version 2; #650's fifteen criteria
**Depth:** Extended. Phase 4.6 (GPT-6 Astra, Gemini 4 Argon) runs after the M7 window's return line (§ Second opinion)

---

## Executive summary

The slice is the recipes, patches and configuration that decide which libretro cores each image carries, how RetroArch is built and patched, and the launch table EmulationStation reads. Three things are now proven, so every finding below is a finding about what ships:

- **The table is the build's own:** the host regeneration of `es_systems.cfg` from the tree's shell is byte-identical to the GENERIC_X64, H700 and SM8550 images.
- **The guest carries the tree:** the installed set on the GENERIC_X64 guest equals the tree's.
- **The rotation tables are the build's:** the five regenerate byte-identically.

**24 findings.** Four are High:

- **#802 (F-08):** the default PlayStation core on H700, RK3326, RK3566 and SM8550, `pcsx_rearmed32`, is installed under `/usr/lib32/libretro` only, while `runemu.sh` loads cores from `/tmp/cores` over `/usr/lib/libretro`. P1 shows that overlay on the guest.
- **F-09:** the next base merge brings upstream's fix for that, and the new `yabadabadoo-lr`, as `build.${DISTRO}-` copies that fail on the fork's `build.${DISTRONAME}` roots. `git merge-tree` merges both cleanly.
- **#806 (F-19):** on the SM8550 image `grep` has no PCRE, through a build-order race in grep's recipe, so `Install Steam.sh`'s `grep -oP` stops Install Steam at its Proton step.
- **F-07:** `aethersx2-sa`, which upstream dropped for a licensing concern, still ships on SM8550. The coordinator has ruled it class 1, with the base merge.

**Medium:**

- `melonds-ds-lr` fetches eleven dependencies from the network at configure time (F-15, blindspot 47's shape);
- `retroarch32` is skipped silently when the ARM root is not exactly one install directory (F-11, latent);
- network data reaches `eval` in `Install Steam.sh` (F-20; M7's `6101242e14` fixes it);
- the Steam runtime and Proton install with no integrity check (F-21);
- the exit hotkey's RPCS3 target names a process that never runs (F-24);
- the table's one-default rule is enforced by accident on one shape and not at all on the other (F-06, T5's code).

**The rest are Low or `Renovation`:** dead options, keys, assignments and flags; a short pin; comments that name a closed issue or a check that does not exist; copies.

**Currency (Q4), the slice's question:**

- **Against ROCKNIX:** all 14 upstream commits on the two directories are absent from `next`, and all are `to merge`, two of them adapted.
- **Against the projects:** of the 151 recipes' pins, 40 are current, 104 behind their project (ROCKNIX's to take), 1 behind ROCKNIX (`np2kai-lr`), and 6 not on their project's default branch.
- **RetroArch:** the pin is 7,623 commits behind master. Against master 15 of the 21 patches fail outright, and two of the fork's eight are already fixed upstream. A bump is a re-cut of the series.

**Time to play** on the image as built: **0.62 s** to the first frame, **1.01 s** game to game (n = 3), beside T1's and T2's. The logging key costs nothing measurable.

## Acceptance-criteria scorecard

At the end of Phase 2 (`02` § Criterion entries): 4 PASS (AC-06, AC-07, AC-08, AC-12), 5 PARTIAL (AC-01, AC-05, AC-09, AC-10, AC-11), 6 open for later stages of this lane (AC-02, AC-03, AC-04, AC-13, AC-14, AC-15), 0 FAIL. This file closes AC-09's, AC-10's and AC-11's gaps (§ Renovation face). AC-01's closes when `05` exists. AC-05 closes at hand-back.

## Code Quality Assessment

### Strengths

- The fork's own lines carry their reasons beside them: `ppsspp-lr`'s strip, `mupen64plus-lr`'s patch set, `idtech-lr`'s pinned data, the rotation hooks.
- The generators fail closed with a reason, and both constructed failures were seen.
- The table guard `Configure retroarch for ${DEVICE}` fails closed.
- The fork's patches 0011 to 0019 each say what they fix and against which report, and 0015's fix is mechanically proven.

### Concerns

- Silent failure paths: `retroarch32` (F-11), `mk_es_systems`' `2>/dev/null` (F-06), grep's autodetected PCRE (#806), cmake's `FetchContent` (F-15).
- Comments that claim what does not happen: F-06, F-18, F-12's `content_history_dir`.

### Complexity Hotspots

- `virtual/emulators/package.mk`'s `makeinstall_target`: one function of about 1,630 lines, three responsibilities (the device lists live above it; the table and the directories in it).
- `Install Steam.sh`: 2,755 lines, five embedded Python programs, logic in several copies (F-23).
- Fifteen `retroarch.cfg` profiles of 817 to 3,530 lines, four of them copies (F-12, Q7).

## Cornerstone Conformance

`03` § 3.2's table: the packaging rules, `device-builds.md`'s merge and fetch lessons, `engineering-practices.md`'s guards and names. **One blindspot was committed in the code (47, F-15)**, and one in this lane's own proof work (74, P3's first launch, caught by its own log).

### Aligned

Every count of the brief re-derived without delta (`01` § Scope). The #336 line is drawn as the brief draws it. The read hash holds.

### Diverged

Seven brief deltas, none a stop (`03` § 3.3):

- 500 against 348 `<core>`;
- 131 against 132 `*-lr` recipes;

## Risk Assessment

| Risk | Severity × impact | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A PlayStation game launched with the default core fails on H700 and SM8550 (#802) | High; the most common console on the main handhelds | `c9820d0e89` adapted to `DISTRONAME` with the base merge; `pcsx_rearmed` (64-bit) is the selectable workaround (the coordinator's FYI to M7) |
| The base merge takes upstream's two `${DISTRO}` copies verbatim and the arm-enabled builds stop (F-09) | High for the merge, none for players | the `git grep -F 'build.${DISTRO}-'` check on the merged tree |
| Install Steam cannot complete on SM8550 (#806) | High; a Tools entry | parse with `python3` (deferred:M7-merge); the grep recipe's `pcre2` dependency (T22's, #669) |
| A licensed-elsewhere package ships (F-07) | High in kind, the coordinator's (#528) | class 1, with the base merge |
| An image's `melonds-ds-lr` built from unpinned code, or a build without network failing (F-15) | Medium | pinned sources and `FETCHCONTENT_FULLY_DISCONNECTED` |
| A future pin bump leaves two install directories, and `retroarch32` vanishes silently (F-11) | Medium, latent | fail loudly on not-exactly-one |
| A table edit lists a `false` core first and two defaults ship (F-06 case B) | Medium, latent; T5's | `.../cores/core[last()]`, `fo` errors printed |

## Coverage Boundary

As `02` § Coverage Boundary. In brief:

- **Read whole or by mechanism:** the fork-own and fork-written units.
- **Read at Q4 and Q6:** the upstream-carried ones.
- **Proven on the guest:** the installed set and RetroArch's start.
- **Proven on the host against three images:** the table and the generators.
- **Not reached:** 32-bit RetroArch, arm builds, devices, the exit hotkey end to end (T1's), the 57 core patches (T42's), the standalone emulators (T30's).

## Finding Verification (Phase 4.5)

Each Critical or High finding, attacked before it is published:

- **F-08 (#802).** Refutation sought: another writer putting `pcsx_rearmed32_libretro.so` under `/usr/lib/libretro` or `/storage/cores` (`git grep -E '/storage/cores|/tmp/cores'`: only the mount and the profiles' keys); `retroarch32` resolving `-L` through `libretro_directory` (`-L` is an absolute path); an overlay with a second lower directory (`tmp-cores.mount` and P1's live `mount` line: `lowerdir=/usr/lib/libretro` only). **None found.** It also stands independently verified: the coordinator verified it on both images before filing #802. What stays a lead: the load failure itself, on an arm device.
- **F-09.** Refutation sought: the fork's roots also existing under the `${DISTRO}` name (`ls -d /workspace/repos/rocknix.worktrees/*/build.ROCKNIX-*`: none on the host; the M7 roots are all `build.pixelelated-*`); `DISTRO` and `DISTRONAME` being equal here (`config/options:24` `ROCKNIX`, `distributions/ROCKNIX/options:17` `pixelelated`). **Survives.**
- **F-19 (#806).** Refutation sought: a PCRE-capable grep earlier on the script's `PATH` (after `source /etc/profile` the `PATH` is `/usr/bin:/usr/sbin`, and `/usr/bin/grep` is the GNU binary); the strings check misreading a PCRE build (the binary links no PCRE library, its size is a third of the PCRE builds', and its root's `config.h` lacks `HAVE_LIBPCRE`, with `config.log` showing why). **Survives.** Independently verified and filed by the coordinator.
- **F-07.** Not a defect claim but a provenance fact, read from `c4d353449e` itself. Survives.

## Renovation face

### Q1 Modernize

| Finding | What is behind | The replacement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F-13 | three `[ $? -eq 0 ] && echo` checks under `set -e` | delete them; the build engine fails the package |
| F-12 | renamed RetroArch keys in every profile (`input_duty_cycle`, `input_turbo_default_button`, `video_hdr_*`, `video_scale_integer_overscale`) and retired ones (`video_layout_*`, `netplay_stateless_mode`) | the pin's own key names, read from `configuration.c` per key at fix time |
| F-23 | dead assignments; logic in copies | Python's `json` for the release parsing (also #806) |
| F-15 | cmake `FetchContent` at configure | pinned sources |

The rotation generators are current Python 3, standard library only, with floors. No Python 2 remnant.

- `pkgcheck` over 152 recipes: one FAIL (F-02, `sameboy-lr`), two WARNs (F-03; `emulators`' `install_script` read and not a finding).
- shellcheck over 30 files: two real errors (F-04), the rest read (`02` § shellcheck).
- One short pin (F-16).
- One patch held by fuzz (F-10).
- Network data in `eval` (F-20).
- The headers keep their credits; the fork's lines carry their reasons.
